### [3] SYSTEM-USER prompt · 2026-06-17 03:49:10 UTC

````
<hypothesis>
kind: hypothesis
title: >-
  The Dual Stratification Hypothesis: How Education and Income Inequality Co-Evolve to Undermine Democratic Resilience
hypothesis: >-
  Among post-1990 democratizers, the interaction between income inequality (measured by SWIID Gini) and education inequality
  (measured by Barro-Lee education Gini) creates a 'dual stratification' equilibrium that accelerates democratic backsliding,
  operating through reduced political equality (V-Dem v2pepwrsoc). Public education spending buffers this effect, but the
  hypothesis requires testing with expanded country sample and valid inequality measures before confirmation. The current
  evidence is limited to correlation patterns in a small sample (3 countries) with poor inequality proxies; confirmatory evidence
  is pending improved measurement and estimation.
motivation: >-
  While recent work establishes income inequality as a predictor of democratic backsliding (PNAS 2025), and Acemoglu & Robinson's
  framework emphasizes de facto vs. de jure power, no study has tested whether education inequality AMPLIFIES income inequality's
  effect on backsliding. This is theoretically important because: (1) Education inequality may matter MORE than income inequality
  for democratic resilience, as education affects political sophistication and who can run for office; (2) The co-evolution
  of both inequalities creates persistent elite capture (Acemoglu & Robinson 2008); (3) Welfare state spending on education
  could break this equilibrium, but this has not been tested at scale. For comparative political economy, this identifies
  a specific mechanism (de facto power distribution) through which inequality undermines democracy, and a specific policy
  lever (education spending) to sustain democratic resilience.
assumptions:
- >-
  Post-1990 democratizers share sufficient institutional variation to identify effects (tested via included country fixed
  effects)
- >-
  V-Dem's political equality index validly measures de facto power distribution (established in V-Dem codebook v.14)
- >-
  Education inequality can be proxied by tertiary enrollment rates and average years of education (direct inequality measures
  by SES not available in OWID, but proxies correlate with inequality in developed democracies)
- >-
  Welfare state spending on education is exogenous to short-term democratic backsliding (tested via lagged specifications)
- >-
  The de facto power mechanism operates at the national level (not just individual level)
investigation_approach: |-
  1. DATA: Merge OWID panels - V-Dem v.14 (liberal democracy index, political equality index), World Bank PIP (Gini coefficients), UNESCO education data (tertiary enrollment, average years), OECD SOCX (public education spending %GDP), Lindert social spending data. Sample: 35-40 post-1990 democratizers, 1990-2024 panel.
  2. IDENTIFICATION: Panel fixed-effects models with lagged dependent variable (Arellano-Bond GMM) to test: (a) Main effect of Gini on V-Dem liberal democracy index; (b) Interaction effect of Gini × education inequality; (c) Mediation via political equality index (Sobel-Goodman test); (d) Moderation via education spending (triple interaction: Gini × education inequality × education spending).
  3. ROBUSTNESS: (a) Alternative inequality measures (SWIID); (b) Alternative democracy measures (Polity V, EIU); (c) Placebo tests (pre-1990 period should show no effect); (d) Instrumental variable approach using lagged education inequality as instrument.
success_criteria: |-
  Hypothesis is CONFIRMED if: (1) Interaction term Gini × education inequality is negative and significant (p < 0.05) for predicting V-Dem liberal democracy decline; (2) Political equality index mediates this relationship (Sobel test p < 0.05); (3) Triple interaction Gini × education inequality × education spending is positive and significant (buffer effect).
  Hypothesis is DISCONFIRMED if: (1) No significant interaction (only main effects); (2) Education spending does not moderate the relationship; (3) De facto power (political equality) does not mediate.
related_works:
- >-
  PNAS 2025 'Income inequality and the erosion of democracy in the twenty-first century' (Haggard, Pennington, et al.): Establishes
  income inequality as predictor of democratic backsliding using V-Dem data. OUR DIFFERENCE: We test education inequality's
  INTERACTION with income inequality, not just main effect of income. Also, we identify mechanism (de facto power) and mediator
  (welfare spending).
- >-
  Acemoglu & Robinson (2008) 'Persistence of Power, Elites, and Institutions' (AER): Theoretically models how de facto power
  offsets de jure political power changes. OUR DIFFERENCE: We provide the first EMPIRICAL TEST of how education inequality
  affects de facto power distribution and subsequent democratic backsliding, using new V-Dem political equality data not available
  when A&R wrote.
- >-
  Baliamoune-Lutz (2018) 'Political Elites, Democracy and Education': Theoretical model on elite's education subsidy decisions
  under de facto power. OUR DIFFERENCE: We test the REVERSE causal direction - how education inequality (not elite's subsidies)
  affects democratic backsliding via de facto power - and use macro panel data across 35+ countries, not just Africa.
- >-
  Iversen & Soskice (2006) 'Education, Inequality, and Social Protection': Links education systems to inequality and welfare
  states. OUR DIFFERENCE: We focus on democratic BACKSLIDING (not just redistribution), use post-1990 democratizers (not just
  OECD), and test de facto power as mechanism (not just political preferences).
- >-
  Zuazu (2018) V-Dem Working Paper 'Electoral Systems and Income Inequality': Tests how political equality (de facto power)
  affects inequality. OUR DIFFERENCE: We reverse the causality - testing how inequality (income + education) affects political
  equality and democratic backsliding, not the other way.
inspiration: >-
  The hypothesis synthesizes three insights from comparative political economy: (1) Acemoglu & Robinson's de facto vs. de
  jure power framework (theoretically established but rarely tested directly with new V-Dem political equality data); (2)
  The 'dual stratification' concept from stratification economics (joint effects of multiple inequalities); (3) The welfare
  state as 'democratic insurance' - extending Iversen & Soskice's skill formation framework to focus on democratic resilience,
  not just redistribution. The cross-domain inspiration comes from 'persistence' models in macroeconomics (how multiple equilibria
  form and what shifts them) applied to political institutions.
terms:
- term: Dual Stratification
  definition: >-
    The co-occurrence of high income inequality and high education inequality, creating a persistent elite-dominated equilibrium
    that resists democratic deepening
- term: De Facto Political Power
  definition: >-
    Political influence arising from wealth, organization, education, or social networks, as distinct from de jure power allocated
    by political institutions (Acemoglu & Robinson 2008)
- term: Political Equality Index (V-Dem)
  definition: >-
    V-Dem measure (v2pepwrsoc) of the extent to which political power is evenly distributed across socioeconomic groups, ranging
    from 0 (elite monopoly) to 4 (equal power)
- term: Democratic Backsliding
  definition: >-
    The state-led debilitation or elimination of political institutions sustaining democratic control over executive power,
    including civil liberties, free elections, and rule of law (Lührmann & Lindberg 2019)
- term: Post-1990 Democratizers
  definition: >-
    Countries that transitioned to democracy after 1989, including post-communist states, Latin American countries in the
    Third Wave, and sub-Saharan African democratizers in the 1990s
- term: Welfare State Buffer
  definition: >-
    The hypothesis that generous welfare state spending (especially on education) reduces the political power asymmetry created
    by income and education inequality, thereby sustaining democratic quality
summary: >-
  Among post-1990 democratizers, the interaction of income and education inequality creates 'dual stratification' that accelerates
  democratic backsliding by enabling elite capture via de facto power. Welfare state education spending breaks this equilibrium.
  Tested using OWID panels (V-Dem, World Bank PIP, UNESCO, OECD SOCX) with panel GMM and mediation analysis.
_relation_rationale: >-
  Refining measurement strategy and acknowledging limited evidence; same conceptual frame but more specific/honest
_confidence_delta: decreased
_key_changes:
- >-
  Added specific measurement requirements: SWIID Gini and Barro-Lee education Gini (per research artifact recommendations)
- >-
  Acknowledged that hypothesis is NOT yet confirmed - current evidence is limited to correlations in small sample with poor
  proxies
- >-
  Added requirement for expanded country sample of post-1990 democratizers (must identify 10-20 countries using V-Dem coding)
- >-
  Clarified that welfare state buffer effect is untested pending valid education inequality measure
- >-
  Added honesty about current limitations: only 3 post-1990 democratizers in current data, high missing data rates
- >-
  Reframed success criteria to require actual GMM estimation with valid measures, not just correlation patterns
relation_type: evolution
</hypothesis>

objective: >-

  standard errors, AR(1)/AR(2) tests, Hansen J test, and instrument counts. Test the dual stratification hypothesis: (a) main
  effect of Gini on V-Dem liberal democracy, (b) interaction effect of Gini × education Gini, (c) mediation via political
  equality index, (d) moderation via education spending (triple interaction).
approach: >-

  iteration's dataset artifact (Barro-Lee education Gini, SWIID Gini, expanded post-1990 democratizer sample); (2) Fallback:
  if current iteration dataset unavailable, use existing dataset art_0LV8JAAyzP55 and note limitations in output; (3) Baseline
  model: v2x_libdem_it = a + b1*v2x_libdem_it-1 + b2*swiid_gini_it + b3*edu_gini_it + b4*(gini*edu_gini)_it + country FE +
  year FE + e_it; (4) Use System GMM (Arellano-Bond) with collapsed instruments to avoid proliferation; (5) Report for each
  model: coefficients, clustered standard errors (by country), AR(1) test (should reject), AR(2) test (should not reject),
  Hansen J test (should not reject), number of instruments; (6) Mediation analysis: Sobel-Goodman test for v2pepwrsoc mediating
  the inequality-democracy link; (7) Moderation analysis: triple interaction with education spending; (8) Robustness: alternative
  democracy measures (Polity V, EIU), placebo tests (pre-1990 period), IV approach with lagged education inequality; (9) Within-country
  analysis: demean data and recompute key correlations to show within-country variation effects. Output: method_out.json with
  complete regression tables ready for paper inclusion.

### [4] HUMAN-USER prompt · 2026-06-17 03:49:10 UTC

```
Direction: Comparative Political Economy — Inequality and Democratic Resilience. Something genuinely novel and groundbreaking that traces how inequality, education, and democratic-quality co-evolve across post-1990 democratizers, identifies what sustains resilience versus backsliding, and tests whether welfare-state institutions mediate the link. MUST use Our World in Data (OWID) panels.

Ambition: level 1 of 5 — confirmatory/parametric science: a careful confirmatory test within established institutional-economics theory; precision, identification, and robustness over conceptual novelty.

Reviewer: I am Daron Acemoglu (MIT). Calibrate from my existing work. Cross-domain perspectives (historical sociology, political behavior, economic anthropology) welcome — but keep them close to what I already know, not too far from my background.

Submission/Goal: a paper for the American Political Science Review, the Journal of Democracy, or World Politics. Audience: comparative political economists and political scientists. Tone: empirically rigorous, grounded in institutional economics, reproducible on public OWID data.
```

```
This is a complex task. I need to create a detailed plan for an EXPERIMENT artifact that will execute the dual stratification hypothesis testing using System GMM estimation. Let me break down what I need to do:

1. **Understand the context**: The hypothesis is about how income inequality and education inequality interact to undermine democratic resilience in post-1990 democratizers. The executor needs to implement System GMM estimation with proper specification.

2. **Review dependencies**: 
   - Dataset artifact (art_0LV8JAAyzP55): Has 1291 country-year observations across 38 countries with V-Dem indices, World Bank Gini, education spending, etc. Only 4 true post-1990 democratizers though.
   - Research artifact (art_fZ1e2VGLwd0n): Provides measurement validity guidance - recommends Barro-Lee education Gini, SWIID for income inequality, V-Dem v2pepwrsoc for political equality, and System GMM with collapsed instruments.

3. **Key requirements from the artifact direction**:
   - Execute full System GMM estimation with proper specification
   - Test: (a) main effect of Gini on V-Dem liberal democracy, (b) interaction effect of Gini × education Gini, (c) mediation via political equality index, (d) moderation via education spending
   - Use linearmodels (System GMM) or build custom GMM estimator
   - Report coefficients, standard errors, AR(1)/AR(2) tests, Hansen J test, instrument counts
   - Mediation analysis: Sobel-Goodman test
   - Moderation analysis: triple interaction with education spending
   - Robustness checks: alternative democracy measures, placebo tests, IV approach
   - Within-country analysis: demean data and recompute correlations

4. **What I need to plan**:


I'll focus on creating a robust analytical framework that addresses the complex interactions between inequality and democratic resilience. The plan must ensure methodological rigor while leveraging the available dataset and research guidance. I'll prioritize a comprehensive approach that captures the nuanced relationships between income inequality, education inequality, and democratic backsliding.

The implementation will require careful consideration of the limited post-1990 democratizer sample and potential measurement challenges. {"content": [{"cache_prompt": false, "type": "text", "text": "Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /home/adrian/projects/ai-inventor/aii_data/users/admin/runs/run_-w6fuC_zXl2B/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_research_1/research_out.json:\n     1\t{\n     2\t  \"title\": \"Measurement Validity and Specification Guidance for Dual Stratification Hypothesis\",\n     3\t  \"layman_summary\": \"This research provides guidance on how to measure education and income inequality and political equality in statistical models testing whether these factors together affect democratic backsliding in countries that democratized after 1990.\",\n     4\t  \"summary\": \"Comprehensive research on measurement validity and GMM specification for panel data analysis of the dual stratification hypothesis. Findings include: (1) Education Gini coefficient from Barro-Lee data is the most valid measure of education inequality, with strong negative correlation to mean years of schooling (r = -0.89); (2) V-Dem v2pepwrsoc (Power distributed by social group) is a valid expert-coded measure of political inequality with good reliability; (3) SWIID is recommended over World Bank PIP for income inequality due to superior comparability and coverage (199 countries, 1960-present); (4) System GMM with collapsed instruments is recommended for interaction terms, treating interaction components as endogenous if any component is endogenous. Detailed specification guidance for xtabond2 in Stata is provided, along with software recommendations and specification test interpretation.\",\n     5\t  \"answer\": \"## Measurement Validity and Specification Guidance for Dual Stratification Hypothesis\\n\\n### Education Inequality Measurement\\n\\n**Recommended Measure**: Education Gini coefficient calculated from Barro-Lee educational attainment data [1][2].\\n\\n**Validity Evidence**:\\n- Thomas, Wang, and Fan (2001) developed education Gini coefficients for 85 countries (1960-1990), establishing that education inequality is negatively associated with average years of schooling [1].\\n- The correlation between education Gini and gender gaps strengthened from 0.53 in the 1970s to 0.69 in the 1990s (both significant), indicating that gender gaps are positively associated with education inequality [1].\\n- High collinearity exists between mean education and education Gini (correlation approximately -0.89, p < 0.001) [1], suggesting that average years of schooling alone is a coarse proxy for education inequality.\\n\\n**Coverage for Post-1990 Democratizers**:\\n- Barro-Lee dataset (2013 version) covers 146 countries from 1950-2010 in 5-year intervals [2].\\n- Updated to 2015 for population aged 15-64, providing good coverage for most post-1990 democratizers.\\n- Some Eastern European and post-Soviet countries may have gaps in early transition years (1989-1995).\\n\\n**Proxy Limitations**:\\n- Tertiary enrollment: Measures access not distribution; poorly captures inequality among lower education groups [1].\\n- Mean years of schooling: High collinearity with education Gini makes it unsuitable as a proxy [1].\\n- Enrollment ratios: Only measure flow, not stock of human capital; problematic for growth models [1].\\n\\n**Recommendation**: Use education Gini coefficient from Thomas et al. (2001) extended dataset or calculate from Barro-Lee (2013) attainment distributions. If unavailable, use secondary school completion rate (lsc) from Barro-Lee as proxy.\\n\\n### V-Dem Political Equality Index Validation\\n\\n**Variable**: v2pepwrsoc - Power distributed by social group (V-Dem v.14) [3].\\n\\n**Definition**:\\n- Question: 'Is political power distributed according to social groups?'\\n- Clarification: Social group differentiated by caste, ethnicity, language, race, region, religion.\\n- Scale: 0 (monopolized by one group) to 4 (all groups have roughly equal power) [3].\\n\\n**Validity Evidence**:\\n- Part of V-Dem's Political Equality component (v2x_egal).\\n- Aggregated using Bayesian factor analysis with v2pepwrses (socioeconomic position) and v2pepwrgen (gender) [3].\\n- Cross-coder reliability: Uses Bayesian Item Response Theory (IRT) model for aggregation.\\n- Factor loadings: v2pepwrsoc has factor loading of 0.511 in social group equality index [3].\\n\\n**Coverage for Post-1990 Democratizers**:\\n- V-Dem covers all countries from 1789-2023 with annual data [3].\\n- Post-1990 democratizers well-covered with multiple expert coders per country-year.\\n\\n**Limitations**:\\n- Subjective expert coding may introduce coder bias.\\n- Social group definitions are contextually defined and may vary across countries.\\n- Not directly comparable to income/education inequality metrics (different scaling).\\n\\n**Recommendation**: Use v2pepwrsoc as primary measure of political inequality. Consider normalizing to 0-1 scale to match other inequality metrics.\\n\\n### Income Inequality Database Comparison: SWIID vs World Bank PIP\\n\\n**SWIID (Standardized World Income Inequality Database)**:\\n- Coverage: 199 countries, 1960 to present [4].\\n- Comparability: Maximizes comparability by standardizing to LIS (Luxembourg Income Study) as standard [4].\\n- Uncertainty estimates: Provides standard errors for each country-year observation.\\n- Validation: Cross-validation against LIS shows superior accuracy vs. alternate datasets [4].\\n- Advantages: Largest coverage, annual data, uncertainty estimates, disposable and market income inequality.\\n\\n**World Bank PIP (Poverty and Inequality Platform)**:\\n- Coverage: Global but mixed welfare measures (income in rich countries, consumption in poor countries) [7].\\n- Limitations: Comparability issues across countries; no before-tax income indicators [7].\\n- Usage: Better for poverty analysis than inequality comparisons.\\n\\n**Comparison**:\\n- SWIID preferred for cross-national inequality research due to standardized methodology [4].\\n- PIP better for within-country poverty analysis.\\n- SWIID uncertainty estimates allow weighting by data quality.\\n\\n**Recommendation**: Use SWIID version 9.92 (April 2026) for income inequality data. Use uncertainty estimates to weight observations if conducting meta-analysis or Bayesian estimation.\\n\\n### Arellano-Bond GMM Specification for Interaction Terms\\n\\n**Specification Guidance**:\\n\\n1. **Model Specification**:\\n   - Baseline difference GMM: \u0394y_it = \u03b1\u0394y_it-1 + \u03b2\u0394x_it + \u03b3\u0394(x_it \u00d7 z_it) + \u0394\u03b5_it\\n   - System GMM augments with level equation: y_it = \u03b1y_it-1 + \u03b2x_it + \u03b3(x_it \u00d7 z_it) + (\u03bc_i + \u03b5_it)\\n\\n2. **Interaction Term Treatment**:\\n   - If component variables (x, z) are endogenous, interaction term (x\u00d7z) should also be treated as endogenous [5].\\n   - Use GMM-style instruments for lagged interaction terms.\\n   - Specify in xtabond2: `iv(x z x_z, laglimits(2 .))` for IV-style or `gmm(x z x_z, laglimits(2 .))` for GMM-style.\\n\\n3. **Instrument Proliferation**:\\n   - Use `collapse` option to limit instrument count.\\n   - Monitor instrument count vs. country count (N).\\n   - Hansen J test should not be rejected (valid instruments).\\n\\n4. **Specification Tests**:\\n   - AR(1) and AR(2) tests: AR(1) should reject, AR(2) should not reject.\\n   - Hansen J test: Tests validity of overidentifying restrictions.\\n   - Difference-in-Hansen tests for subsets of instruments.\\n\\n5. **Software Implementation**:\\n   - Stata: xtabond2 (Roodman 2006) [5].\\n   - R: pgmm package (plm).\\n   - Python: linearmodels package.\\n\\n**Applied Examples from Political Economy**:\\n- Acemoglu et al. (2019) 'Democracy Does Cause Growth' uses difference and system GMM with lagged GDP as endogenous, instrumented with deeper lags [6].\\n- Specification controls for country fixed effects and rich GDP dynamics to address dip before democratization [6].\\n\\n**Recommendation**: Use system GMM (more efficient than difference GMM) with collapsed instruments. Treat interaction terms as endogenous if component variables are endogenous. Ensure T \u2265 3 for difference GMM, more for system GMM.\\n\\n### Alternative Data Sources\\n\\n**Education Spending**:\\n- OECD SOCX (Social Expenditure Database): 38 OECD countries, 1980-2022.\\n- UNESCO Institute for Statistics: Global coverage but limited time series.\\n- Lindert (2004) historical social spending data: Some post-1990 countries covered.\\n\\n**Education Inequality Alternatives**:\\n- World Inequality Database on Education (WIDE): Household survey data, limited country coverage.\\n- Demographic and Health Surveys (DHS): Education inequality by gender, within-country detail.\\n- PISA/GSEM test scores: Learning inequality, but only recent years and developed countries.\\n\\n**Recommendation**: Primary analysis use Barro-Lee for education inequality, SWIID for income inequality, V-Dem for political equality. Supplementary analysis can use OECD SOCX for education spending (OECD countries only).\\n\\n### Limitations and Caveats\\n\\n1. **Education Inequality**: \\n   - Education Gini not available in OWID panels directly; must merge from external source.\\n   - Barro-Lee data in 5-year intervals; may need interpolation for annual analysis.\\n\\n2. **Income Inequality**:\\n   - SWIID is model-based; uncertainty increases for countries with fewer source observations.\\n   - Consumption-based measures (developing countries) not strictly comparable to income-based (developed countries).\\n\\n3. **Political Equality**:\\n   - V-Dem expert coding may have subjectivity bias.\\n   - Annual changes may be noisy; consider 3-year moving average.\\n\\n4. **GMM Assumptions**:\\n   - Requires T \u2265 3 for difference GMM, more for system GMM.\\n   - Instrument validity depends on no serial correlation beyond AR(2).\\n   - Results sensitive to instrument choice; conduct robustness checks.\\n\\n### Follow-Up Questions\\n\\n1. How should education inequality be measured when Barro-Lee data has gaps for post-1990 democratizers in early transition years (1989-1995)?\\n2. What is the appropriate treatment of interaction terms when one component is time-invariant (e.g., colonial heritage, legal origin)?\\n3. How should standard errors be adjusted when using OWID panels that may have serial correlation beyond AR(2)?\",\n     6\t  \"sources\": [\n     7\t    {\n     8\t      \"index\": 1,\n     9\t      \"url\": \"https://openknowledge.worldbank.org/entities/publication/50558cd4-59e3-5742-b78e-7a4afac5fbf7\",\n    10\t      \"title\": \"Measuring Education Inequality: Gini Coefficients of Education (Thomas, Wang, & Fan, 2001)\",\n    11\t      \"summary\": \"Foundational paper developing education Gini coefficients for 85 countries, showing negative association between education inequality and average years of schooling, and validating the measure against enrollment-based indicators.\"\n    12\t    },\n    13\t    {\n    14\t      \"index\": 2,\n    15\t      \"url\": \"http://barrolee.com/\",\n    16\t      \"title\": \"Barro-Lee Educational Attainment Dataset (2013)\",\n    17\t      \"summary\": \"Provides educational attainment data for 146 countries from 1950-2010, which is the underlying data source for calculating education Gini coefficients.\"\n    18\t    },\n    19\t    {\n    20\t      \"index\": 3,\n    21\t      \"url\": \"https://v-dem.net/documents/38/V-Dem_Codebook_v14.pdf\",\n    22\t      \"title\": \"V-Dem Codebook v.14 (2024)\",\n    23\t      \"summary\": \"Documents V-Dem variables including v2pepwrsoc (Power distributed by social group), with question wording, response scales, and aggregation methodology using Bayesian IRT models.\"\n    24\t    },\n    25\t    {\n    26\t      \"index\": 4,\n    27\t      \"url\": \"https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/abs/10.1111/ssqu.12795\",\n    28\t      \"title\": \"Measuring Income Inequality Across Countries and Over Time: The Standardized World Income Inequality Database (Solt, 2020)\",\n    29\t      \"summary\": \"Validates SWIID against LIS, showing superior comparability and coverage.   "file_text": "# Experiment Plan: System GMM Estimation of Dual Stratification Hypothesis\n\n## Title\nSystem GMM Estimation of Inequality Interaction Effects on Democratic Backsliding: Testing the Dual Stratification Hypothesis\n\n## Summary\nExecute full System GMM (Arellano-Bond) panel estimation to test whether the interaction of income and education inequality affects democratic backsliding, with mediation via political equality and moderation via education spending. Produces publication-ready regression tables with diagnostic tests.\n\n---\n\n## PHASE 0: Setup and Data Loading (30 min)\n\n### Step 0.1: Check for iter_2 dataset artifact\n```python\nimport json, os, pandas as pd, numpy as np\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\n# Check multiple possible locations for iter_2 dataset\nITER_2_DATASET_PATHS = [\n    \"/home/adrian/projects/ai-inventor/aii_data/users/admin/runs/run_-w6fuC_zXl2B/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_1/data_out_complete.json\",\n    \"/home/adrian/projects/ai-inventor/aii_data/users/admin/runs/run_-w6fuC_zXl2B/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_1/data_out.json\",\n]\nITER_1_DATASET_PATH = \"/home/adrian/projects/ai-inventor/aii_data/users/admin/runs/run_-w6fuC_zXl2B/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_1/data_out_complete.json\"\n\n# Load data: prefer iter_2, fallback to iter_1\ndf = None\nDATA_SOURCE = \"unknown\"\nfor p in ITER_2_DATASET_PATHS:\n    if os.path.exists(p):\n        with open(p) as f:\n            data = json.load(f)\n        df = pd.DataFrame(data)\n        DATA_SOURCE = f\"iter_2: {p}\"\n        print(f\"Using iter_2 dataset: {p}\")\n        break\n\nif df is None:\n    with open(ITER_1_DATASET_PATH) as f:\n        data = json.load(f)\n    df = pd.DataFrame(data)\n    DATA_SOURCE = \"iter_1_fallback\"\n    print(f\"WARNING: Using iter_1 fallback dataset. SWIID/Barro-Lee data not available.\")\n```\n\n### Step 0.2: Attempt to augment data with SWIID and Barro-Lee\n```python\n# ATTEMPT 1: Check if swiid Python package is available\ntry:\n    # SWIID may be available via pip: pip install swiid\n    # If not available, download from: https://www.fredericksolt.com/research/swiid/\n    import requests\n    swiid_url = \"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/fsolt/swiid/master/data/swiid.csv\"\n    resp = requests.get(swiid_url, timeout=30)\n    if resp.status_code == 200:\n        swiid_df = pd.read_csv(pd.io.common.StringIO(resp.text))\n        print(f\"SWIID data downloaded: {len(swiid_df)} rows\")\n        # Merge on country and year\n        # SWIID columns: country, year, gini_net, gini_market, etc.\n        df = df.merge(swiid_df[['country', 'year', 'gini_net']], \n                      on=['country', 'year'], how='left', suffixes=('', '_swiid'))\n        df['gini_swiid'] = df['gini_net'] * 100  # SWIID is 0-1, WB is 0-100\nexcept Exception as e:\n    print(f\"SWIID download failed: {e}. Using World Bank Gini.\")\n\n# ATTEMPT 2: Barro-Lee education Gini\ntry:\n    # Barro-Lee data: http://www.barrolee.com/\n    # Look for education Gini in the dataset\n    barrolee_url = \"http://barrolee.com/data/BL2013_MF1599_v2.1.csv\"\n    resp = requests.get(barrolee_url, timeout=30)\n    if resp.status_code == 200:\n        bl_df = pd.read_csv(pd.io.common.StringIO(resp.text))\n        print(f\"Barro-Lee data downloaded: {len(bl_df)} rows\")\n        # Process to create education Gini or use mean years of schooling\n        # Barro-Lee has: country, year, male_edu, female_edu, total_edu (mean years)\nexcept Exception as e:\n    print(f\"Barro-Lee download failed: {e}. Using tertiary enrollment proxy.\")\n```\n\n### Step 0.3: Data preprocessing\n```python\n# Ensure correct types\ndf['year'] = df['year'].astype(int)\ndf['country'] = df['country'].astype(str)\n\n# Create panel variables\ndf = df.sort_values(['country', 'year']).reset_index(drop=True)\n\n# Create lagged dependent variable\ndf['v2x_libdem_lag'] = df.groupby('country')['v2x_libdem'].shift(1)\n\n# Create interaction term: Gini \u00d7 education inequality\n# IF swiid gini available: use gini_swiid, else use gini (World Bank)\n# IF Barro-Lee education Gini available: use edu_gini_bl, else use edu_ineq_index\ngini_var = 'gini_swiid' if 'gini_swiid' in df.columns else 'gini'\nedu_var = 'edu_gini_bl' if 'edu_gini_bl' in df.columns else 'edu_ineq_index'\n\ndf['gini_edu_interaction'] = df[gini_var] * df[edu_var]\n\n# Create triple interaction: Gini \u00d7 education inequality \u00d7 education spending\ndf['triple_interaction'] = df[gini_var] * df[edu_var] * df['education_spending_gdp']\n\n# Create within-country demeaned variables for within estimator comparison\nfor col in [gini_var, edu_var, 'gini_edu_interaction', 'v2x_libdem', 'v2pepwrsoc', 'education_spending_gdp', 'triple_interaction']:\n    if col in df.columns:\n        country_mean = df.groupby('country')[col].transform('mean')\n        df[f'{col}_within'] = df[col] - country_mean\n\n# Subset to post-1990 democratizers for main analysis\npost1990 = df[df['post_1990_democratizer'] == True].copy()\nprint(f\"Post-1990 democratizers: {post1990['country'].nunique()} countries, {len(post1990)} observations\")\nprint(f\"Countries: {post1990['country'].unique()}\")\n\n# Also create full sample for comparison\nfull_sample = df.copy()\n```\n\n---\n\n## PHASE 1: System GMM Estimation (2h)\n\n### Step 1.1: Install and import required packages\n```python\n# REQUIRED PACKAGES (install with uv pip install):\n# linearmodels, statsmodels, pandas, numpy, scipy, matplotlib, seaborn\n# For mediation: pingouin (or implement Sobel test manually)\n\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\nfrom linearmodels.panel import PanelOLS, RandomEffects, BetweenOLS\nfrom linearmodels.panel.gmm import SystemGMM, DifferenceGMM, IVGMM\nimport statsmodels.api as sm\nimport statsmodels.formula.api as smf\nfrom scipy import stats\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\n```\n\n### Step 1.2: Implement System GMM with linearmodels\n\n**CRITICAL NOTE**: `linearmodels.SystemGMM` requires careful specification. The executor must handle these issues:\n1. `SystemGMM` uses formula API or array API\n2. Interaction terms must be created as columns before estimation\n3. Instrument proliferation: use `instrument_lags` parameter judiciously\n4. AR(1)/AR(2) tests: compute manually from residuals\n5. Hansen J test: available in `results.hansen`\n\n```python\nfrom linearmodels.panel.gmm import SystemGMM\nfrom linearmodels.panel.data import PanelData\n\ndef estimate_system_gmm(df, y_var, x_vars, endogenous_vars=None, \n                         lag_limits=(2, 10), collapse_instruments=True):\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate System GMM with proper specification.\n    \n    Parameters:\n    -----------\n    df : pandas DataFrame with 'country' and 'year' columns\n    y_var : str, dependent variable (e.g., 'v2x_libdem')\n    x_vars : list, exogenous and predetermined variables\n    endogenous_vars : list, variables to treat as endogenous (instrumented with lags)\n    lag_limits : tuple, (min_lag, max_lag) for instruments\n    collapse_instruments : bool, whether to collapse instrument matrix\n    \n    Returns:\n    --------\n    results : SystemGMM results object\n    diagnostics : dict with AR(1), AR(2), Hansen J, instrument count\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    # Prepare data\n    df_gmm = df[['country', 'year', y_var] + x_vars].dropna().copy()\n    df_gmm = df_gmm.set_index(['country', 'year'])\n    \n    # Create lagged dependent variable\n    y_lag = df_gmm.groupby(level='country')[y_var].shift(1)\n    X = df_gmm[x_vars]\n    Y = df_gmm[y_var]\n    \n    # For SystemGMM, specify:\n    # - dependent: y_var\n    # - exog: exogenous variables (including time dummies)\n    # - endog: endogenous variables (instrumented with lags)\n    # - instruments: deep lags of endogenous variables\n    \n    # Create time fixed effects (year dummies)\n    years = df_gmm.index.get_level_values('year')\n    year_dummies = pd.get_dummies(years, prefix='year')\n    year_dummies.index = df_gmm.index\n    \n    # Full specification\n    X_with_fe = pd.concat([X, year_dummies], axis=1)\n    \n    # SPECIFY MODEL\n    # Note: linearmodels SystemGMM uses different syntax than Stata's xtabond2\n    # The `formulas` approach is most readable\n    \n    # Build formula\n    endog_vars = endogenous_vars if endogenous_vars else []\n    exog_vars = [v for v in x_vars if v not in endog_vars]\n    \n    # For interaction terms, they should be treated as endogenous if either component is endogenous\n    # Specify in the model\n    \n    raise NotImplementedError(\"See Step 1.3 for actual implementation approach\")\n```\n\n### Step 1.3: Alternative implementation - use pgmm from linearmodels correctly\n\n**REVISED APPROACH**: `linearmodels.SystemGMM` has a complex API. The executor should use this tested approach:\n\n```python\nfrom linearmodels.panel.gmm import SystemGMM\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef run_gmm_estimation(df, model_spec):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run System GMM with proper error handling.\n    \n    model_spec dict:\n        - y: dependent variable name\n        - x_exog: list of exogenous variables (time-invariant or predetermined)\n        - x_endog: list of endogenous variables (to be instrumented with lags)\n        - interaction_vars: list of interaction terms (treat as endogenous)\n        - time_fe: bool, include year fixed effects\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    # 1. SET UP PANEL INDEX\n    df_idx = df.set_index(['country', 'year']).copy()\n    \n    # 2. CREATE Y AND X MATRICES\n    y = df_idx[model_spec['y']]\n    \n    # All x variables (exogenous + endogenous + interactions)\n    x_cols = model_spec.get('x_exog', []) + model_spec.get('x_endog', []) + model_spec.get('interaction_vars', [])\n    if model_spec.get('time_fe', True):\n        year_dummies = pd.get_dummies(df_idx.index.get_level_values('year'), prefix='year')\n        year_dummies.index = df_idx.index\n        X = pd.concat([df_idx[x_cols], year_dummies], axis=1)\n    else:\n        X = df_idx[x_cols]\n    \n    # 2.5 CREATE LAGGED DEPENDENT VARIABLE (essential for dynamic panel)\n    y_lag = df_idx.groupby(level='country')[model_spec['y']].shift(1)\n    X = pd.concat([y_lag, X], axis=1)\n    X = X.rename(columns={y_lag.name: f\"{model_spec['y']}_lag1\"})\n    \n    # 3. SPECIFY INSTRUMENTS\n    # For System GMM: use deep lags (>=2) of endogenous variables as instruments\n    # linearmodels uses `instrument_lags` parameter\n    \n    # 4. ESTIMATE\n    model = SystemGMM(y, X, \n                      entity_effects=True,  # Country fixed effects (via demeaning)\n                      time_effects=False,\n                      # For endogenous vars, specify instrument lags\n                      )\n    \n    # Actually, linearmodels.SystemGMM needs `formula` or specific instrument spec\n    # Let's use the formula API which is clearer:\n    \n    # BUILD FORMULA\n    y_lag_name = f\"{model_spec['y']}_lag1\"\n    exog_formula = ' + '.join(model_spec.get('x_exog', []))\n    endog_formula = ' + '.join(model_spec.get('x_endog', []))\n    \n    # For instrumental variable specification in formula:\n    # endog_vars ~ exog_vars | instruments\n    # Example: \"v2x_libdem ~ v2x_libdem_lag + gini + education_spending [gini + gini_lag1 + gini_lag2]\"\n    \n    # THIS IS COMPLEX. See Step 1.4 for the recommended approach using Stata-like syntax\n    # or using a simpler estimator first (Difference GMM) then System GMM.\n    \n    results = model.fit(cov_type='kernel', debiased=True)\n    return results\n```\n\n### Step 1.4: RECOMMENDED APPROACH - Use simpler dynamic panel first, then System GMM\n\nGiven the complexity of `linearmodels.SystemGMM` with interaction terms, the executor should:\n\n**APPROACH A: Two-step estimation (recommended for this project)**\n1. Estimate simple dynamic panel models using `PanelOLS` with lagged DV (includes country FE)\n2. Use `DifferenceGMM` (Arellano-Bond) as implemented in linearmodels\n3. Compare results across estimators\n\n**APPROACH B: Use the `pgmm` function from linearmodels correctly**\n```python\nfrom linearmodels.panel.gmm import DifferenceGMM, SystemGMM\n\n# Difference GMM (Arellano-Bond)\n# Formula: dependent ~ lagged_dependent + exog | instruments\n# For endogenous variables, instruments are deep lags\n\nmodel = DifferenceGMM.from_formula(\n    formula='v2x_libdem ~ 1 + v2x_libdem_lag + gini + edu_ineq_index + gini_edu_interaction + education_spending_gdp | '\n            'v2x_libdem_lag + gini_lag2 + gini_lag3 + edu_ineq_index_lag2 + edu_ineq_index_lag3',\n    data=df_idx,\n    weight_type='kernel'\n)\nresults = model.fit()\n```\n\n**APPROACH C: Stata-like implementation via subprocess (fallback)**\nIf linearmodels proves too limited, write Stata .do file and run via subprocess (if Stata available), or use R's `pgmm` via rpy2.\n\n### Step 1.5: SPECIFICATION OF FOUR MODELS\n\n```python\n# ============================================================================\n# MODEL 1: Main effect of Gini on V-Dem liberal democracy\n# ============================================================================\nMODEL1_SPEC = {\n    'y': 'v2x_libdem',\n    'x_exog': ['education_spending_gdp'],  # exogenous\n    'x_endog': ['gini'],  # endogenous: instrument with lags\n    'interaction_vars': [],\n    'description': 'Model 1: Main effect of income inequality on liberal democracy'\n}\n\n# ============================================================================\n# MODEL 2: Interaction effect of Gini \u00d7 education inequality\n# ============================================================================\nMODEL2_SPEC = {\n    'y': 'v2x_libdem',\n    'x_exog': ['education_spending_gdp'],\n    'x_endog': ['gini', 'edu_ineq_index'],  # both endogenous\n    'interaction_vars': ['gini_edu_interaction'],\n    'description': 'Model 2: Interaction of income and education inequality'\n}\n\n# ============================================================================\n# MODEL 3: Mediation via political equality (separate model, not GMM)\n# Step 1: gini_edu_interaction -> v2pepwrsoc (political equality)\n# Step 2: v2pepwrsoc -> v2x_libdem (controlling for gini_edu_interaction)\n# Mediation: Sobel-Goodman test\n# ============================================================================\nMODEL3_SPEC = {\n    'mediator': 'v2pepwrsoc',\n    'y': 'v2x_libdem',\n    'x': ['gini', 'edu_ineq_index', 'gini_edu_interaction'],\n    'description': 'Model 3: Mediation via political equality index'\n}\n\n# ============================================================================\n# MODEL 4: Triple interaction with education spending (moderation)\n# ============================================================================\nMODEL4_SPEC = {\n    'y': 'v2x_libdem',\n    'x_exog': [],\n    'x_endog': ['gini', 'edu_ineq_index'],\n    'interaction_vars': ['gini_edu_interaction', 'triple_interaction'],\n    'description': 'Model 4: Triple interaction with education spending'\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## PHASE 2: Diagnostic Tests (1h)\n\n### Step 2.1: AR(1) and AR(2) tests\n```python\ndef ar_test(residuals, max_lag=2):\n    \"\"\"\n    Arellano-Bond serial correlation test.\n    Tests H0: no serial correlation of order `lag` in first differences of residuals.\n    \n    For valid GMM:\n    - AR(1) should be significant (p < 0.05): confirms first-differencing removed unit root\n    - AR(2) should NOT be significant (p > 0.05): confirms no residual serial correlation\n    \n    Implementation: Regress residuals on lagged residuals, test significance of coefficient.\n    \"\"\"\n    resid_diff = np.diff(residuals)  # First differences of residuals\n    \n    tests = {}\n    for lag in [1, 2]:\n        if lag == 1:\n            y = resid_diff[1:]\n            X = resid_diff[:-1]\n        else:  # lag == 2\n            y = resid_diff[2:]\n            X = resid_diff[:-2]\n        \n        X = sm.add_constant(X)\n        model = sm.OLS(y, X).fit()\n        tests[f'AR({lag})'] = {\n            'coeff': model.params[1],\n            'se': model.bse[1],\n            'p_value': model.pvalues[1],\n            'n_obs': len(y)\n        }\n    \n    return tests\n\n# Apply to each model's residuals\nfor model_name, results in all_results.items():\n    residuals = results.resids\n    ar_results = ar_test(residuals)\n    print(f\"{model_name} AR tests:\")\n    print(f\"  AR(1) p-value: {ar_results['AR(1)']['p_value']:.4f} (should be < 0.05)\")\n    print(f\"  AR(2) p-value: {ar_results['AR(2)']['p_value']:.4f} (should be > 0.05)\")\n```\n\n### Step 2.2: Hansen J test (overidentification test)\n```python\n# linearmodels stores Hansen J test in results.hansen\nfor model_name, results in all_results.items():\n    if hasattr(results, 'hansen'):\n        hansen = results.hansen\n        print(f\"{model_name} Hansen J test:\")\n        print(f\"  Statistic: {hansen.stat:.4f}\")\n        print(f\"  p-value: {hansen.pval:.4f} (should be > 0.05 for valid instruments)\")\n    else:\n        print(f\"{model_name}: Hansen J test not available\")\n```\n\n### Step 2.3: Instrument count and proliferation check\n```python\nfor model_name, results in all_results.items():\n    n_instruments = results.instrument_count if hasattr(results, 'instrument_count') else 'N/A'\n    n_countries = len(results.included_variables)  # approximate\n    print(f\"{model_name}:\")\n    print(f\"  Instruments: {n_instruments}\")\n    print(f\"  Countries: {n_countries}\")\n    if isinstance(n_instruments, (int, float)) and n_instruments > n_countries:\n        print(f\"  WARNING: Instrument proliferation! Instruments ({n_instruments}) > Countries ({n_countries})\")\n```\n\n---\n\n## PHASE 3: Mediation Analysis (1h)\n\n### Step 3.1: Implement Sobel-Goodman mediation test\n```python\nfrom scipy import stats as sci_stats\n\ndef sobel_goodman_test(df, x, m, y, control_vars=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Sobel-Goodman mediation test.\n    \n    Tests whether mediator M carries the influence of X to Y.\n    \n    Steps:\n    1. Regress Y on X (total effect): Y = a*X + e1\n    2. Regress M on X (effect of X on M): M = b*X + e2  \n    3. Regress Y on X and M (direct effect): Y = c'*X + d*M + e3\n    \n    Mediation effect = b * d\n    Sobel test: z = b*d / sqrt(b^2*se_d^2 + d^2*se_b^2)\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    # Prepare data\n    vars_needed = [x, m, y] + (control_vars or [])\n    df_med = df[vars_needed].dropna().copy()\n    \n    # Step 1: Total effect (c)\n    X_total = sm.add_constant(df_med[x])\n    model_total = sm.OLS(df_med[y], X_total).fit()\n    c = model_total.params[x]  # total effect\n    se_c = model_total.bse[x]\n    \n    # Step 2: Effect of X on M (b)\n    X_med = sm.add_constant(df_med[x])\n    model_med = sm.OLS(df_med[m], X_med).fit()\n    b = model_med.params[x]  # effect of X on mediator\n    se_b = model_med.bse[x]\n    \n    # Step 3: Direct effect (c') and mediator effect (d)\n    X_dir = sm.add_constant(df_med[[x, m]])\n    model_dir = sm.OLS(df_med[y], X_dir).fit()\n    cp = model_dir.params[x]  # direct effect c'\n    d = model_dir.params[m]   # effect of mediator on Y\n    se_d = model_dir.bse[m]\n    \n    # Sobel test\n    mediation_effect = b * d\n    se_sobel = np.sqrt(b**2 * se_d**2 + d**2 * se_b**2)\n    z_sobel = mediation_effect / se_sobel\n    p_sobel = 2 * (1 - sci_stats.norm.cdf(abs(z_sobel)))\n    \n    # Goodman test (similar, slightly different SE)\n    se_goodman = np.sqrt(b**2 * se_d**2 + d**2 * se_b**2 + se_b**2 * se_d**2)\n    z_goodman = mediation_effect / se_goodman\n    p_goodman = 2 * (1 - sci_stats.norm.cdf(abs(z_goodman)))\n    \n    return {\n        'total_effect': c,\n        'direct_effect': cp,\n        'mediation_effect': mediation_effect,\n        'prop_mediated': mediation_effect / c if c != 0 else None,\n        'sobel_z': z_sobel,\n        'sobel_p': p_sobel,\n        'goodman_z': z_goodman,\n        'goodman_p': p_goodman,\n        'b_coef': b,\n        'b_se': se_b,\n        'd_coef': d,\n        'd_se': se_d,\n        'n_obs': len(df_med)\n    }\n\n# Apply to Model 3\nmediation_results = sobel_goodman_test(\n    df=df,\n    x='gini_edu_interaction',\n    m='v2pepwrsoc',\n    y='v2x_libdem',\n    control_vars=['education_spending_gdp']\n)\nprint(\"Mediation Analysis (Sobel-Goodman):\")\nprint(f\"  Mediation effect: {mediation_results['mediation_effect']:.4f}\")\nprint(f\"  Proportion mediated: {mediation_results['prop_mediated']:.2%}\")\nprint(f\"  Sobel z: {mediation_results['sobel_z']:.4f}, p: {mediation_results['sobel_p']:.4f}\")\n```\n\n### Step 3.2: Bootstrap confidence intervals for mediation effect\n```python\ndef bootstrap_mediation(df, x, m, y, n_bootstrap=1000):\n    \"\"\"Bootstrap CI for mediation effect.\"\"\"\n    mediation_effects = []\n    \n    for i in range(n_bootstrap):\n        df_boot = df.sample(frac=1, replace=True)\n        try:\n            result = sobel_goodman_test(df_boot, x, m, y)\n            mediation_effects.append(result['mediation_effect'])\n        except:\n            pass\n    \n    mediation_effects = np.array(mediation_effects)\n    ci_lower = np.percentile(mediation_effects, 2.5)\n    ci_upper = np.percentile(mediation_effects, 97.5)\n    \n    return ci_lower, ci_upper, mediation_effects\n```\n\n---\n\n## PHASE 4: Robustness Checks (1.5h)\n\n### Step 4.1: Alternative democracy measures\n```python\n# Check if alternative democracy measures are available in dataset\n# If not, download from V-Dem directly\n\nALTERNATIVE_DEMOCRACY_MEASURES = [\n    'v2x_polyarchy',  # Electoral democracy index\n    'v2x_freexp_altinf',  # Freedom of expression index\n    'v2x_rol',  # Rule of law index\n]\n\nfor measure in ALTERNATIVE_DEMOCRACY_MEASURES:\n    if measure in df.columns:\n        print(f\"Estimating Model 2 with {measure} as dependent variable...\")\n        # Re-run Model 2 with alternative DV\n        # Compare coefficient signs and significance\n```\n\n### Step 4.2: Placebo test (pre-1990 period)\n```python\n# If pre-1990 data is available (V-Dem starts at 1789):\n# 1. Subset to 1975-1989 (pre-democratization wave)\n# 2. Re-run Model 2\n# 3. EXPECTED: No significant interaction effect (placebo)\n\ndf_pre1990 = df[(df['year'] >= 1975) & (df['year'] <= 1989)].copy()\nif len(df_pre1990) > 0:\n    print(\"Running placebo test (pre-1990 period)...\")\n    # Run Model 2 on df_pre1990\n    # The interaction term should NOT be significant\n```\n\n### Step 4.3: Within-country analysis (demeaned data)\n```python\ndef within_estimator(df, y_var, x_vars):\n    \"\"\"\n    Within estimator (fixed effects via demeaning).\n    Compares with GMM results to check consistency.\n    \"\"\"\n    df_w = df.copy()\n    \n    # Demean all variables by country\n    for col in [y_var] + x_vars:\n        if col in df_w.columns:\n            country_mean = df_w.groupby('country')[col].transform('mean')\n            df_w[f'{col}_within'] = df_w[col] - country_mean\n    \n    # OLS on demeaned data (no constant = country FE absorbed)\n    Y_w = df_w[f'{y_var}_within']\n    X_w = df_w[[f'{v}_within' for v in x_vars]]\n    X_w = sm.add_constant(X_w)  # Still need constant for within model\n    \n    model_w = sm.OLS(Y_w, X_w).fit()\n    return model_w\n\n# Compare within estimator with GMM\nwithin_results = within_estimator(df, 'v2x_libdem', \n                                  ['gini', 'edu_ineq_index', 'gini_edu_interaction'])\nprint(\"Within estimator results:\")\nprint(within_results.summary())\n```\n\n### Step 4.4: IV approach with lagged education inequality\n```python\n# Instrument education inequality with its lag\n# Education inequality is likely persistent; lag is valid instrument if no direct effect on democracy\n\nfrom linearmodels.iv import IV2SLS\n\ndef iv_estimation(df, y_var, x_vars, instruments):\n    \"\"\"IV estimation using 2SLS.\"\"\"\n    df_iv = df[[y_var] + x_vars + instruments].dropna().copy()\n    df_iv = df_iv.set_index(['country', 'year']) if 'country' in df_iv.columns else df_iv\n    \n    # First stage: instrumented_var ~ instruments + other_x_vars\n    # Second stage: y ~ predicted_instrumented_var + other_x_vars\n    \n    # Use linearmodels IV2SLS\n    formula = f\"{y_var} ~ 1 + {' + '.join([v for v in x_vars if v not in instruments])} + [{' + '.join(instruments)} ~ {' + '.join([f'{v}_lag1' for v in instruments])}]\"\n    # Simplified: just use lag as instrument directly\n    \n    for var_to_instrument in instruments:\n        iv_var = f'{var_to_instrument}_lag1'\n        df_iv[iv_var] = df_iv.groupby('country')[var_to_instrument].shift(1)\n        \n        model_iv = IV2SLS(df_iv[y_var], \n                          df_iv[[v for v in x_vars if v != var_to_instrument] + [iv_var]],\n                          df_iv[var_to_instrument],\n                          df_iv[iv_var])\n        results_iv = model_iv.fit()\n    \n    return results_iv\n```\n\n---\n\n## PHASE 5: Output Generation (1h)\n\n### Step 5.1: Create method_out.json with complete results\n```python\ndef create_regression_table(results_dict, diagnostics_dict):\n    \"\"\"\n    Create publication-ready regression table.\n    \n    Returns dict with:\n    - models: list of model results (coefficients, SEs, p-values, N)\n    - diagnostics: AR(1), AR(2), Hansen J, instrument count for each model\n    - mediation: mediation analysis results\n    - robustness: robustness check results\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    output = {\n        'metadata': {\n            'dataset_source': DATA_SOURCE,\n            'sample': 'post-1990 democratizers' if USE_POST1990_ONLY else 'full_sample',\n            'n_countries': df['country'].nunique(),\n            'n_observations': len(df),\n            'years': f\"{df['year'].min()}-{df['year'].max()}\",\n            'estimation_method': 'System GMM (Arellano-Bond)',\n            'software': 'Python linearmodels',\n            'notes': [\n                'Standard errors clustered by country',\n                'Year fixed effects included',\n                'Country fixed effects handled via GMM transformation'\n            ]\n        },\n        'models': {},\n        'diagnostics': {},\n        'mediation_analysis': {},\n        'robustness_checks': {},\n        'hypothesis_test_results': {\n            'confirmed': None,  # Will be set based on results\n            'reason': ''\n        }\n    }\n    \n    # Populate models\n    for model_name, results in results_dict.items():\n        output['models'][model_name] = {\n            'coefficients': results.params.to_dict() if hasattr(results, 'params') else {},\n            'standard_errors': results.std_errors.to_dict() if hasattr(results, 'std_errors') else {},\n            'p_values': results.pvalues.to_dict() if hasattr(results, 'pvalues') else {},\n            'n_obs': int(results.nobs) if hasattr(results, 'nobs') else None,\n            'r_squared': float(results.rsquared) if hasattr(results, 'rsquared') else None,\n        }\n    \n    # Populate diagnostics\n    output['diagnostics'] = diagnostics_dict\n    \n    # Mediation\n    output['mediation_analysis'] = mediation_results\n    \n    return output\n\n# Save output\nmethod_out = create_regression_table(all_results, all_diagnostics)\nmethod_out_path = Path(__file__).parent / 'method_out.json'\nwith open(method_out_path, 'w') as f:\n    json.dump(method_out, f, indent=2, default=str)\nprint(f\"Results saved to {method_out_path}\")\n```\n\n### Step 5.2: Determine if hypothesis is confirmed\n```python\ndef evaluate_hypothesis(results_dict, mediation_results):\n    \"\"\"\n    Evaluate success criteria from artifact_direction:\n    \n    CONFIRMED if:\n    1. Interaction term Gini \u00d7 education inequality is negative and significant (p < 0.05)\n    2. Political equality index mediates (Sobel test p < 0.05)\n    3. Triple interaction with education spending is positive and significant (buffer effect)\n    \n    DISCONFIRMED if:\n    1. No significant interaction (only main effects)\n    2. Education spending does not moderate\n    3. De facto power (political equality) does not mediate\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    model2 = results_dict.get('Model 2: Interaction', None)\n    model4 = results_dict.get('Model 4: Triple interaction', None)\n    \n    # Criterion 1: Interaction term significant and negative\n    if model2 is not None:\n        interaction_coef = model2.params.get('gini_edu_interaction', None)\n        interaction_p = model2.pvalues.get('gini_edu_interaction', None)\n        criterion1 = (interaction_coef is not None and \n                      interaction_p is not None and \n                      interaction_p < 0.05 and \n                      interaction_coef < 0)\n    else:\n        criterion1 = False\n    \n    # Criterion 2: Mediation significant\n    criterion2 = (mediation_results.get('sobel_p', 1) < 0.05)\n    \n    # Criterion 3: Triple interaction positive and significant\n    if model4 is not None:\n        triple_coef = model4.params.get('triple_interaction', None)\n        triple_p = model4.pvalues.get('triple_interaction', None)\n        criterion3 = (triple_coef is not None and \n                      triple_p is not None and \n                      triple_p < 0.05 and \n                      triple_coef > 0)\n    else:\n        criterion3 = False\n    \n    confirmed = criterion1 and criterion2 and criterion3\n    \n    return {\n        'confirmed': confirmed,\n        'criterion1': criterion1,\n        'criterion2': criterion2,\n        'criterion3': criterion3,\n        'reason': f\"Criteria: interaction={criterion1}, mediation={criterion2}, triple_int={criterion3}\"\n    }\n\nevaluation = evaluate_hypothesis(all_results, mediation_results)\nmethod_out['hypothesis_test_results'] = evaluation\n```\n\n---\n\n## FALLBACK PLAN\n\nIf System GMM fails or produces invalid results:\n\n### Fallback 1: Use Panel OLS with lagged DV (biased but consistent)\n```python\nfrom linearmodels.panel import PanelOLS\n\ndef panel_ols_with_lag(df, y_var, x_vars):\n    \"\"\"Panel OLS with lagged DV and fixed effects.\"\"\"\n    df_idx = df.set_index(['country', 'year'])\n    y_lag = df_idx.groupby(level='country')[y_var].shift(1)\n    X = pd.concat([df_idx[x_vars], y_lag], axis=1)\n    X = X.dropna()\n    Y = df_idx.loc[X.index][y_var]\n    \n    model = PanelOLS(Y, X, entity_effects=True, time_effects=True)\n    results = model.fit(cov_type='clustered', cluster_entity=True)\n    return results\n\n# Note: This estimator is biased for the lagged DV coefficient (Nickell bias)\n# but consistent for N->inf, T fixed. OK for large N.\n```\n\n### Fallback 2: Use Difference GMM (simpler than System GMM)\n```python\nfrom linearmodels.panel.gmm import DifferenceGMM\n\nmodel = DifferenceGMM.from_formula(\n    formula='v2x_libdem ~ 1 + v2x_libdem_lag + gini + edu_ineq_index + gini_edu_interaction | '\n            'v2x_libdem_lag + [gini + edu_ineq_index ~ gini_lag2 + edu_ineq_index_lag2]',\n    data=df_idx\n)\nresults = model.fit()\n```\n\n### Fallback 3: Use simple OLS with clustered SEs (report as limited evidence)\n```python\n# Cluster standard errors by country\n# Use `cluster.se` from df['country']})\n```\n\n---\n\n## TESTING PLAN\n\n### Test 1: Mini dataset smoke test (first 5 countries, 10 years each)\n```python\n# Before running full estimation, test with subset\ntest_countries countries\")\n\n# Run Model 1 on test data\n# Check that: no errors, coefficients are finite, SEs are positive\n```\n\n### Test 2: Verify interaction term creation\n```python\n# Check that interaction See Step 1.4 for the recommended approach using Stata-like syntax\n    # or using a simpler estimator first (Difference GMM) then System GMM.\n    \n    results = model.fit(cov_type='kernel', debiased=True)\n    return results\n```\n\n### Step 1.4: RECOMMENDED APPROACH - Use simpler dynamic panel first, then System GMM\n\nGiven the complexity of `linearmodels.SystemGMM` with interaction terms, the executor should:\n\n**APPROACH A: Two-step estimation (recommended for this project)**\n1. Estimate simple dynamic panel models using `PanelOLS` with lagged DV (includes country FE)\n2. Use `DifferenceGMM` (Arellano-Bond) as implemented in linearmodels\n3. Compare results across estimators\n\n**APPROACH B: Use the `pgmm` function from linearmodels correctly**\n```python\nfrom linearmodels.panel.gmm import DifferenceGMM, SystemGMM\n\n# Difference GMM (Arellano-Bond)\n# Formula: dependent ~ lagged_dependent + exog | instruments\n# For endogenous variables, instruments are deep lags\n\nmodel = DifferenceGMM.from_formula(\n    formula='v2x_libdem ~ 1 + v2x_libdem_lag + gini + edu_ineq_index + gini_edu_interaction + education_spending_gdp | '\n            'v2x_libdem_lag + gini_lag2 + gini_lag3 + edu_ineq_index_lag2 + edu_ineq_index_lag3',\n    data=df_idx,\n    weight_type='kernel'\n)\nresults = model.fit()\n```\n\n**APPROACH C: Stata-like implementation via subprocess (fallback)**\nIf linearmodels proves too limited, write Stata .do file and run via subprocess (if Stata available), or use R's `pgmm` via rpy2.\n\n### Step 1.5: SPECIFICATION OF FOUR MODELS\n\n```python\n# ============================================================================\n# MODEL 1: Main effect of Gini on V-Dem liberal democracy\n# ============================================================================\nMODEL1_SPEC = {\n    'y': 'v2x_libdem',\n    'x_exog': ['education_spending_gdp'],  # exogenous\n    'x_endog': ['gini'],  # endogenous: instrument with lags\n    'interaction_vars': [],\n    'description': 'Model 1: Main effect of income inequality on liberal democracy'\n}\n\n# ============================================================================\n# MODEL 2: Interaction effect of Gini \u00d7 education inequality\n# ============================================================================\nMODEL2_SPEC = {\n    'y': 'v2x_libdem',\n    'x_exog': ['education_spending_gdp'],\n    'x_endog': ['gini', 'edu_ineq_index'],  # both endogenous\n    'interaction_vars': ['gini_edu_interaction'],\n    'description': 'Model 2: Interaction of income and education inequality'\n}\n\n# ============================================================================\n# MODEL 3: Mediation via political equality (separate model, not GMM)\n# Step 1: gini_edu_interaction -> v2pepwrsoc (political equality)\n# Step 2: v2pepwrsoc -> v2x_libdem (controlling for gini_edu_interaction)\n# Mediation: Sobel-Goodman test\n# ============================================================================\nMODEL3_SPEC = {\n    'mediator': 'v2pepwrsoc',\n    'y': 'v2x_libdem',\n    'x': ['gini', 'edu_ineq_index', 'gini_edu_interaction'],\n    'description': 'Model 3: Mediation via political equality index'\n}\n\n# ============================================================================\n# MODEL 4: Triple interaction with education spending (moderation)\n# ============================================================================\nMODEL4_SPEC = {\n    'y': 'v2x_libdem',\n    'x_exog': [],\n    'x_endog': ['gini', 'edu_ineq_index'],\n    'interaction_vars': ['gini_edu_interaction', 'triple_interaction'],\n    'description': 'Model 4: Triple interaction with education spending'\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## PHASE 2: Diagnostic Tests (1h)\n\n### Step 2.1: AR(1) and AR(2) tests\n```python\ndef ar_test(residuals, max_lag=2):\n    \"\"\"\n    Arellano-Bond serial correlation test.\n    Tests H0: no serial correlation of order `lag` in first differences of residuals.\n    \n    For valid GMM:\n    - AR(1) should be significant (p < 0.05): confirms first-differencing removed unit root\n    - AR(2) should NOT be significant (p > 0.05): confirms no residual serial correlation\n    \n    Implementation: Regress residuals on lagged residuals, test significance of coefficient.\n    \"\"\"\n    resid_diff = np.diff(residuals)  # First differences of residuals\n    \n    tests = {}\n    for lag in [1, 2]:\n        if lag == 1:\n            y = resid_diff[1:]\n            X = resid_diff[:-1]\n        else:  # lag == 2\n            y = resid_diff[2:]\n            X = resid_diff[:-2]\n        \n        X = sm.add_constant(X)\n        model = sm.OLS(y, X).fit()\n        tests[f'AR({lag})'] = {\n            'coeff': model.params[1],\n            'se': model.bse[1],\n            'p_value': model.pvalues[1],\n            'n_obs': len(y)\n        }\n    \n    return tests\n\n# Apply to each model's residuals\nfor model_name, results in all_results.items():\n    residuals = results.resids\n    ar_results = ar_test(residuals)\n    print(f\"{model_name} AR tests:\")\n    print(f\"  AR(1) p-value: {ar_results['AR(1)']['p_value']:.4f} (should be < 0.05)\")\n    print(f\"  AR(2) p-value: {ar_results['AR(2)']['p_value']:.4f} (should be > 0.05)\")\n```\n\n### Step 2.2: Hansen J test (overidentification test)\n```python\n# linearmodels stores Hansen J test in results.hansen\nfor model_name, results in all_results.items():\n    if hasattr(results, 'hansen'):\n        hansen = results.hansen\n        print(f\"{model_name} Hansen J test:\")\n        print(f\"  Statistic: {hansen.stat:.4f}\")\n        print(f\"  p-value: {hansen.pval:.4f} (should be > 0.05 for valid instruments)\")\n    else:\n        print(f\"{model_name}: Hansen J test not available\")\n```\n\n### Step 2.3: Instrument count and proliferation check\n```python\nfor model_name, results in all_results.items():\n    n_instruments = results.instrument_count if hasattr(results, 'instrument_count') else 'N/A'\n    n_countries = len(results.included_variables)  # approximate\n    print(f\"{model_name}:\")\n    print(f\"  Instruments: {n_instruments}\")\n    print(f\"  Countries: {n_countries}\")\n    if isinstance(n_instruments, (int, float)) and n_instruments > n_countries:\n        print(f\"  WARNING: Instrument proliferation! Instruments ({n_instruments}) > Countries ({n_countries})\")\n```\n\n---\n\n## PHASE 3: Mediation Analysis (1h)\n\n### Step 3.1: Implement Sobel-Goodman mediation test\n```python\nfrom scipy import stats as sci_stats\n\ndef sobel_goodman_test(df, x, m, y, control_vars=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Sobel-Goodman mediation test.\n    \n    Tests whether mediator M carries the influence of X to Y.\n    \n    Steps:\n    1. Regress Y on X (total effect): Y = a*X + e1\n    2. Regress M on X (effect of X on M): M = b*X + e2  \n    3. Regress Y on X and M (direct effect): Y = c'*X + d*M + e3\n    \n    Mediation effect = b * d\n    Sobel test: z = b*d / sqrt(b^2*se_d^2 + d^2*se_b^2)\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    # Prepare data\n    vars_needed = [x, m, y] + (control_vars or [])\n    df_med = df[vars_needed].dropna().copy()\n    \n    # Step 1: Total effect (c)\n    X_total = sm.add_constant(df_med[x])\n    model_total = sm.OLS(df_med[y], X_total).fit()\n    c = model_total.params[x]  # total effect\n    se_c = model_total.bse[x]\n    \n    # Step 2: Effect of X on M (b)\n    X_med = sm.add_constant(df_med[x])\n    model_med = sm.OLS(df_med[m], X_med).fit()\n    b = model_med.params[x]  # effect of X on mediator\n    se_b = model_med.bse[x]\n    \n    # Step 3: Direct effect (c') and mediator effect (d)\n    X_dir = sm.add_constant(df_med[[x, m]])\n    model_dir = sm.OLS(df_med[y], X_dir).fit()\n    cp = model_dir.params[x]  # direct effect c'\n    d = model_dir.params[m]   # effect of mediator on Y\n    se_d = model_dir.bse[m]\n    \n    # Sobel test\n    mediation_effect = b * d\n    se_sobel = np.sqrt(b**2 * se_d**2 + d**2 * se_b**2)\n    z_sobel = mediation_effect / se_sobel\n    p_sobel = 2 * (1 - sci_stats.norm.cdf(abs(z_sobel)))\n    \n    # Goodman test (similar, slightly different SE)\n    se_goodman = np.sqrt(b**2 * se_d**2 + d**2 * se_b**2 + se_b**2 * se_d**2)\n    z_goodman = mediation_effect / se_goodman\n    p_goodman = 2 * (1 - sci_stats.norm.cdf(abs(z_goodman)))\n    \n    return {\n        'total_effect': c,\n        'direct_effect': cp,\n        'mediation_effect': mediation_effect,\n        'prop_mediated': mediation_effect / c if c != 0 else None,\n        'sobel_z': z_sobel,\n        'sobel_p': p_sobel,\n        'goodman_z': z_goodman,\n        'goodman_p': p_goodman,\n        'b_coef': b,\n        'b_se': se_b,\n        'd_coef': d,\n        'd_se': se_d,\n        'n_obs': len(df_med)\n    }\n\n# Apply to Model 3\nmediation_results = sobel_goodman_test(\n    df=df,\n    x='gini_edu_interaction',\n    m='v2pepwrsoc',\n    y='v2x_libdem',\n    control_vars=['education_spending_gdp']\n)\nprint(\"Mediation Analysis (Sobel-Goodman):\")\nprint(f\"  Mediation effect: {mediation_results['mediation_effect']:.4f}\")\nprint(f\"  Proportion mediated: {mediation_results['prop_mediated']:.2%}\")\nprint(f\"  Sobel z: {mediation_results['sobel_z']:.4f}, p: {mediation_results['sobel_p']:.4f}\")\n```\n\n### Step 3.2: Bootstrap confidence intervals for mediation effect\n```python\ndef bootstrap_mediation(df, x, m, y, n_bootstrap=1000):\n    \"\"\"Bootstrap CI for mediation effect.\"\"\"\n    mediation_effects = []\n    \n    for i in range(n_bootstrap):\n        df_boot = df.sample(frac=1, replace=True)\n        try:\n            result = sobel_goodman_test(df_boot, x, m, y)\n            mediation_effects.append(result['mediation_effect'])\n        except:\n            pass\n    \n    mediation_effects = np.array(mediation_effects)\n    ci_lower = np.percentile(mediation_effects, 2.5)\n    ci_upper = np.percentile(mediation_effects, 97.5)\n    \n    return ci_lower, ci_upper, mediation_effects\n```\n\n---\n\n## PHASE 4: Robustness Checks (1.5h)\n\n### Step 4.1: Alternative democracy measures\n```python\n# Check if alternative democracy measures are available in dataset\n# If not, download from V-Dem directly\n\nALTERNATIVE_DEMOCRACY_MEASURES = [\n    'v2x_polyarchy',  # Electoral democracy index\n    'v2x_freexp_altinf',  # Freedom of expression index\n    'v2x_rol',  # Rule of law index\n]\n\nfor measure in ALTERNATIVE_DEMOCRACY_MEASURES:\n    if measure in df.columns:\n        print(f\"Estimating Model 2 with {measure} as dependent variable...\")\n        # Re-run Model 2 with alternative DV\n        # Compare coefficient signs and significance\n```\n\n### Step 4.2: Placebo test (pre-1990 period)\n```python\n# If pre-1990 data is available (V-Dem starts at 1789):\n# 1. Subset to 1975-1989 (pre-democratization wave)\n# 2. Re-run Model 2\n# 3. EXPECTED: No significant interaction effect (placebo)\n\ndf_pre1990 = df[(df['year'] >= 1975) & (df['year'] <= 1989)].copy()\nif len(df_pre1990) > 0:\n    print(\"Running placebo test (pre-1990 period)...\")\n    # Run Model 2 on df_pre1990\n    # The interaction term should NOT be significant\n```\n\n### Step 4.3: Within-country analysis (demeaned data)\n```python\ndef within_estimator(df, y_var, x_vars):\n    \"\"\"\n    Within estimator (fixed effects via demeaning).\n    Compares with GMM results to check consistency.\n    \"\"\"\n    df_w = df.copy()\n    \n    # Demean all variables by country\n    for col in [y_var] + x_vars:\n        if col in df_w.columns:\n            country_mean = df_w.groupby('country')[col].transform('mean')\n            df_w[f'{col}_within'] = df_w[col] - country_mean\n    \n    # OLS on demeaned data (no constant = country FE absorbed)\n    Y_w = df_w[f'{y_var}_within']\n    X_w = df_w[[f'{v}_within' for v in x_vars]]\n    X_w = sm.add_constant(X_w)  # Still need constant for within model\n    \n    model_w = sm.OLS(Y_w, X_w).fit()\n    return model_w\n\n# Compare within estimator with GMM\nwithin_results = within_estimator(df, 'v2x_libdem', \n                                  ['gini', 'edu_ineq_index', 'gini_edu_interaction'])\nprint(\"Within estimator results:\")\nprint(within_results.summary())\n```\n\n### Step 4.4: IV approach with lagged education inequality\n```python\n# Instrument education inequality with its lag\n# Education inequality is likely persistent; lag is valid instrument if no direct effect on democracy\n\nfrom linearmodels.iv import IV2SLS\n\ndef iv_estimation(df, y_var, x_vars, instruments):\n    \"\"\"IV estimation using 2SLS.\"\"\"\n    df_iv = df[[y_var] + x_vars + instruments].dropna().copy()\n    df_iv = df_iv.set_index(['country', 'year']) if 'country' in df_iv.columns else df_iv\n    \n    # First stage: instrumented_var ~ instruments + other_x_vars\n    # Second stage: y ~ predicted_instrumented_var + other_x_vars\n    \n    # Use linearmodels IV2SLS\n    formula = f\"{y_var} ~ 1 + {' + '.join([v for v in x_vars if v not in instruments])} + [{' + '.join(instruments)} ~ {' + '.join([f'{v}_lag1' for v in instruments])}]\"\n    # Simplified: just use lag as instrument directly\n    \n    for var_to_instrument in instruments:\n        iv_var = f'{var_to_instrument}_lag1'\n        df_iv[iv_var] = df_iv.groupby('country')[var_to_instrument].shift(1)\n        \n        model_iv = IV2SLS(df_iv[y_var], \n                          df_iv[[v for v in x_vars if v != var_to_instrument] + [iv_var]],\n                          df_iv[var_to_instrument],\n                          df_iv[iv_var])\n        results_iv = model_iv.fit()\n    \n    return results_iv\n```\n\n---\n\n## PHASE 5: Output Generation (1h)\n\n### Step 5.1: Create method_out.json with complete results\n```python\ndef create_regression_table(results_dict, diagnostics_dict):\n    \"\"\"\n    Create publication-ready regression table.\n    \n    Returns dict with:\n    - models: list of model results (coefficients, SEs, p-values, N)\n    - diagnostics: AR(1), AR(2), Hansen J, instrument count for each model\n    - mediation: mediation analysis results\n    - robustness: robustness check results\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    output = {\n        'metadata': {\n            'dataset_source': DATA_SOURCE,\n            'sample': 'post-1990 democratizers' if USE_POST1990_ONLY else 'full_sample',\n            'n_countries': df['country'].nunique(),\n            'n_observations': len(df),\n            'years': f\"{df['year'].min()}-{df['year'].max()}\",\n            'estimation_method': 'System GMM (Arellano-Bond)',\n            'software': 'Python linearmodels',\n            'notes': [\n                'Standard errors clustered by country',\n                'Year fixed effects included',\n                'Country fixed effects handled via GMM transformation'\n            ]\n        },\n        'models': {},\n        'diagnostics': {},\n        'mediation_analysis': {},\n        'robustness_checks': {},\n        'hypothesis_test_results': {\n            'confirmed': None,  # Will be set based on results\n            'reason': ''\n        }\n    }\n    \n    # Populate models\n    for model_name, results in results_dict.items():\n        output['models'][model_name] = {\n            'coefficients': results.params.to_dict() if hasattr(results, 'params') else {},\n            'standard_errors': results.std_errors.to_dict() if hasattr(results, 'std_errors') else {},\n            'p_values': results.pvalues.to_dict() if hasattr(results, 'pvalues') else {},\n            'n_obs': int(results.nobs) if hasattr(results, 'nobs') else None,\n            'r_squared': float(results.rsquared) if hasattr(results, 'rsquared') else None,\n        }\n    \n    # Populate diagnostics\n    output['diagnostics'] = diagnostics_dict\n    \n    # Mediation\n    output['mediation_analysis'] = mediation_results\n    \n    return output\n\n# Save output\nmethod_out = create_regression_table(all_results, all_diagnostics)\nmethod_out_path = Path(__file__).parent / 'method_out.json'\nwith open(method_out_path, 'w') as f:\n    json.dump(method_out, f, indent=2, default=str)\nprint(f\"Results saved to {method_out_path}\")\n```\n\n### Step 5.2: Determine if hypothesis is confirmed\n```python\ndef evaluate_hypothesis(results_dict, mediation_results):\n    \"\"\"\n    Evaluate success criteria from artifact_direction:\n    \n    CONFIRMED if:\n    1. Interaction term Gini \u00d7 education inequality is negative and significant (p < 0.05)\n    2. Political equality index mediates (Sobel test p < 0.05)\n    3. Triple interaction with education spending is positive and significant (buffer effect)\n    \n    DISCONFIRMED if:\n    1. No significant interaction (only main effects)\n    2. Education spending does not moderate\n    3. De facto power (political equality) does not mediate\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    model2 = results_dict.get('Model 2: Interaction', None)\n    model4 = results_dict.get('Model 4: Triple interaction', None)\n    \n    # Criterion 1: Interaction term significant and negative\n    if model2 is not None:\n        interaction_coef = model2.params.get('gini_edu_interaction', None)\n        interaction_p = model2.pvalues.get('gini_edu_interaction', None)\n        criterion1 = (interaction_coef is not None and \n                      interaction_p is not None and \n                      interaction_p < 0.05 and \n                      interaction_coef < 0)\n    else:\n        criterion1 = False\n    \n    # Criterion 2: Mediation significant\n    criterion2 = (mediation_results.get('sobel_p', 1) < 0.05)\n    \n    # Criterion 3: Triple interaction positive and significant\n    if model4 is not None:\n        triple_coef = model4.params.get('triple_interaction', None)\n        triple_p = model4.pvalues.get('triple_interaction', None)\n        criterion3 = (triple_coef is not None and \n                      triple_p is not None and \n                      triple_p < 0.05 and \n                      triple_coef > 0)\n    else:\n        criterion3 = False\n    \n    confirmed = criterion1 and criterion2 and criterion3\n    \n    return {\n        'confirmed': confirmed,\n        'criterion1': criterion1,\n        'criterion2': criterion2,\n        'criterion3': criterion3,\n        'reason': f\"Criteria: interaction={criterion1}, mediation={criterion2}, triple_int={criterion3}\"\n    }\n\nevaluation = evaluate_hypothesis(all_results, mediation_results)\nmethod_out['hypothesis_test_results'] = evaluation\n```\n\n---\n\n## FALLBACK PLAN\n\nIf System GMM fails or produces invalid results:\n\n### Fallback 1: Use Panel OLS with lagged DV (biased but consistent)\n```python\nfrom linearmodels.panel import PanelOLS\n\ndef panel_ols_with_lag(df, y_var, x_vars):\n    \"\"\"Panel OLS with lagged DV and fixed effects.\"\"\"\n    df_idx = df.set_index(['country', 'year'])\n    y_lag = df_idx.groupby(level='country')[y_var].shift(1)\n    X = pd.concat([df_idx[x_vars], y_lag], axis=1)\n    X = X.dropna()\n    Y = df_idx.loc[X.index][y_var]\n    \n    model = PanelOLS(Y, X, entity_effects=True, time_effects=True)\n    results = model.fit(cov_type='clustered', cluster_entity=True)\n    return results\n\n# Note: This estimator is biased for the lagged DV coefficient (Nickell bias)\n# but consistent for N->inf, T fixed. OK for large N.\n```\n\n### Fallback 2: Use Difference GMM (simpler than System GMM)\n```python\nfrom linearmodels.panel.gmm import DifferenceGMM\n\nmodel = DifferenceGMM.from_formula(\n    formula='v2x_libdem ~ 1 + v2x_libdem_lag + gini + edu_ineq_index + gini_edu_interaction | '\n            'v2x_libdem_lag + [gini + edu_ineq_index ~ gini_lag2 + edu_ineq_index_lag2]',\n    data=df_idx\n)\nresults = model.fit()\n```\n\n### Fallback 3: Use simple OLS with clustered SEs (report as limited evidence)\n```python\n# Cluster standard errors by country\n# Use `cluster.se` from `linearmodels` or `statsmodels`\n\nmodel = smf.ols('v2x_libdem ~ gini * edu_ineq_index + education_spending_gdp', data=df)\nresults = model.fit(cov_type='cluster', cov_kwds={'groups': df['country']})\n```\n\n---\n\n## TESTING PLAN\n\n### Test 1: Mini dataset smoke test (first 5 countries, 10 years each)\n```python\n# Before running full estimation, test with subset\ntest_countries = df['country'].unique()[:5]\ndf_test = df[df['country'].isin(test_countries) & (df['year'] >= 2000)].copy()\nprint(f\"Smoke test: {len(df_test)} observations, {df_test['country'].nunique()} countries\")\n\n# Run Model 1 on test data\n# Check that: no errors, coefficients are finite, SEs are positive\n```\n\n### Test 2: Verify interaction term creation\n```python\n# Check that interaction term is correctly computed\nassert np.allclose(df['gini_edu_interaction'], df['gini'] * df['edu_ineq_index'])\nprint(\"Interaction term correctly computed.\")\n```\n\n### Test 3: Verify post-1990 democratizer subsample\n```python\npost1990 = df[df['post_1990_democratizer'] == True]\nprint(f\"Post-1990 democratizers: {post1990['country'].nunique()} countries\")\nprint(f\"Countries: {list(post1990['country'].unique())}\")\n# If < 10 countries: WARNING, may not have sufficient power for subgroup analysis\n```\n\n---\n\n## KEY FILE PATHS\n\n**Input files:**\n- Iter 2 dataset (if exists): `/home/adrian/projects/ai-inventor/aii_data/users/admin/runs/run_-w6fuC_zXl2B/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_1/data_out_complete.json`\n- Iter 1 fallback dataset:
